# Claim 77 — A third of excess deaths in the United States between 1 March and  1 August 2020 during the COVID-19 pandemic could not be directly attributed to the coronavirus

## Initial Q&A

### What official public health data source provides the total number of recorded deaths in the United States for the period between 1 March and 1 August 2020?
Answer: The official public health data source cited in the research is the **National Center for Health Statistics**, with the study's authors stating they "analyzed death data from the National Center for Health Statistics and the US Census Bureau."

Source URL: https://www.cnn.com/2020/10/12/health/us-deaths-covid-19-pandemic-jama-study/index.html

### What statistical method and historical data are used by public health authorities (such as the CDC) to calculate the "expected" or baseline number of deaths in the United States for a given time period, such as March-August?
Answer: The CDC calculates the expected number of deaths using "a statistical model based on the previous three years of mortality data, incorporating seasonal trends as well as adjustments for data-reporting delays."

Source URL: https://theconversation.com/279-700-extra-deaths-in-the-us-so-far-in-this-pandemic-year-147887

### Based on the official death data and the calculated baseline, what was the total number of excess deaths in the United States between 1 March and 1 August 2020?
Answer: According to the study, there were **225,530 excess deaths** in the United States between March 1 and August 1, 2020. The source states: "Of the 225,530 excess deaths, 150,541 – or 67% of them – were attributed to Covid-19."

Source URL: https://www.cnn.com/2020/10/12/health/us-deaths-covid-19-pandemic-jama-study/index.html

### According to official death certificate data, how many deaths in the United States between 1 March and 1 August 2020 had COVID-19 listed as the underlying or a contributing cause of death?
Answer: According to the JAMA study cited in the search result, 150,541 deaths were attributed to COVID-19 between March 1 and August 1, 2020. The article states: "Of the 225,530 excess deaths, 150,541 – or 67% of them – were attributed to Covid-19."

Source URL: https://www.cnn.com/2020/10/12/health/us-deaths-covid-19-pandemic-jama-study/index.html

### Is there a recognized published study or official analysis that explicitly calculates the proportion of excess deaths in the United States during the specified period that were *not* attributed to COVID-19 on death certificates?
Answer: Yes. A peer-reviewed study published in **JAMA** by researchers from Virginia Commonwealth University and Yale University explicitly calculated this proportion. The study analyzed National Center for Health Statistics and U.S. Census Bureau data and found that "COVID-19 was a documented cause of only 67% of these excess deaths," meaning approximately **33% (one-third) of excess deaths were not attributed to COVID-19** on death certificates. Specifically, out of 225,530 excess deaths during the study period, 150,541 were attributed to COVID-19, leaving roughly 75,000 excess deaths unaccounted for by COVID-19 as a documented cause. The lead author, Dr. Steven Woolf, noted that some of these additional deaths likely resulted from pandemic-related disruptions—such as untreated chronic diseases, delayed emergency care, and emotional crises leading to overdoses or suicides—rather than direct COVID-19 infection.

Source URL: https://www.foxnews.com/health/coronavirus-us-excess-deaths-study

### If such a study exists, what was the exact figure or percentage it reported for excess deaths not attributed to COVID-19?
Answer: According to the study, exactly 33% of excess deaths between March and August 2020 were not attributed to COVID-19, as "COVID-19 was a documented cause of only 67% of these excess deaths."

Source URL: https://www.foxnews.com/health/coronavirus-us-excess-deaths-study

### What was the defined methodology in that study for categorizing a death as "directly attributed" versus "not directly attributed" to the coronavirus?
Answer: The study defined a death as "directly attributed" to COVID-19 based on whether COVID-19 was assigned as the cause of death on the death certificate; deaths not listing COVID-19 as the cause, even if they were excess deaths beyond expected levels, were categorized as "not directly attributed." Specifically, "26% of all excess deaths were not directly attributed to COVID," meaning these "did not directly assign COVID as the cause of death" on their death certificates.

Source URL: https://penntoday.upenn.edu/news/US-covid-deaths-may-be-underestimated-36-percent

### Does the study account for potential misclassification, delays in reporting, or deaths where COVID-19 may have been an undiagnosed factor?
Answer: Yes, the study accounts for these factors by using CDC data, which adjusts for reporting delays and notes that excess deaths exceed COVID-19-attributed deaths, suggesting potential undercounting or misclassification. As the source states, CDC models incorporate "adjustments for data-reporting delays," and researchers identified that "COVID-19 was documented as a cause of death in 67% of excess deaths," implying the remainder may involve undiagnosed cases or other pandemic-related causes.

Source URL: https://theconversation.com/279-700-extra-deaths-in-the-us-so-far-in-this-pandemic-year-147887

### Has any subsequent analysis or peer review of the study's methodology or conclusions produced conflicting results regarding the proportion of non-COVID-19 excess deaths?
Answer: The search result does not indicate any direct conflict from subsequent peer review; instead, it cites a JAMA study that found "COVID-19 was documented as a cause of death in 67% of excess deaths between March and July in the U.S.," which aligns with the claim that about a third (33%) were not directly attributed to the coronavirus, while also suggesting some of these may be undercounted COVID-19 deaths or indirect pandemic effects.

Source URL: https://theconversation.com/279-700-extra-deaths-in-the-us-so-far-in-this-pandemic-year-147887

### Did the original claim correctly report the exact figure, time frame, and geographic scope (United States) as presented in the source study or analysis?
Answer: **Yes**, the claim correctly reports the exact figure, time frame, and geographic scope as presented in the source study. The source states that "Covid-19 was a documented cause of only 67% of these excess deaths" during "March 1 through August 1" in "the United States," meaning approximately one-third (33%) of excess deaths could *not* be directly attributed to the coronavirus. The claim's use of "a third," its date range of "1 March and 1 August 2020," and its specification of "the United States" all align precisely with the source study's findings as reported by CNN.

Source URL: https://www.cnn.com/2020/10/12/health/us-deaths-covid-19-pandemic-jama-study/index.html

# Claim 79 — Anthony Weiner’s laptop contained proof Hillary Clinton & her associates are involved in child trafficking & paedophilia

## Initial Q&A

### 1. Was a laptop computer belonging to Anthony Weiner officially seized or examined by law enforcement or investigative authorities?
Answer: Yes, a laptop computer belonging to Anthony Weiner was officially seized and examined by law enforcement. According to the search result, "on October 3, 2016, New York FBI agents executed a search warrant to obtain Weiner's iPhone, an iPad and the laptop." The laptop was then examined by the FBI, which led to the discovery of approximately 650,000 emails, including emails between Hillary Clinton and Huma Abedin. When these emails were found to be potentially relevant to a separate investigation, "the U.S. Department of Justice reportedly applied for a search warrant for the Abedin/Clinton emails, which was apparently authorized by a federal judge," and the FBI subsequently reviewed the emails on the laptop.

Source URL: https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/hidden-legal-lessons-from-anthony-weiners-laptop-computer-stark

### 2. If the laptop was seized, which specific law enforcement agency or authority had custody of it and conducted the examination?
Answer: The laptop was seized and examined by the **FBI** (Federal Bureau of Investigation). Specifically, "on October 3, 2016, New York FBI agents executed a search warrant to obtain Weiner’s iPhone, an iPad and the laptop," and the FBI subsequently conducted the review of its contents.

Source URL: https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/hidden-legal-lessons-from-anthony-weiners-laptop-computer-stark

### 3. During the examination of Anthony Weiner's laptop, did authorities discover files or communications that they determined constituted evidence of Hillary Clinton's personal involvement in child trafficking or pedophilia?
Answer: Based on the provided source, the examination of Anthony Weiner's laptop did not lead authorities to discover evidence of Hillary Clinton's personal involvement in child trafficking or pedophilia. The source details that the FBI discovered emails pertinent to the prior investigation into mishandling of classified information, but after a review, the FBI director stated the investigation's conclusions remained unchanged, indicating no criminal charges were warranted. The article focuses entirely on the legal and procedural aspects of the laptop's seizure and search, with no mention of findings related to trafficking or pedophilia.

Source URL: https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/hidden-legal-lessons-from-anthony-weiners-laptop-computer-stark

### 4. Did any official investigation, such as one conducted by the FBI, conclude that the contents of Anthony Weiner's laptop provided proof that Hillary Clinton was involved in child trafficking or pedophilia?
Answer: No official investigation has concluded that the contents of Anthony Weiner's laptop provided proof of Hillary Clinton's involvement in child trafficking or pedophilia. The FBI's review of emails found on the laptop pertained to whether they contained classified information related to Clinton's use of a private email server, and after completing its review on November 6, 2016, the FBI stated it had "not changed our conclusions" — meaning no charges were recommended. The Justice Department Inspector General's June 2018 report found no evidence that the laptop was "deliberately placed on the back-burner by others in the FBI to protect Clinton," but likewise found no evidence linking its contents to child trafficking or pedophilia. The claim conflates Weiner's separate investigation for "sexting" with an underage girl with unsubstantiated allegations against Clinton, none of which were supported by any law enforcement finding.

Source URL: https://www.factcheck.org/2018/08/clintons-emails-weiners-laptop-and-a-falsehood/

### 5. Have any federal, state, or local prosecutors in the United States brought criminal charges against Hillary Clinton based on evidence found on Anthony Weiner's laptop?
Answer: No, based on the search result, no criminal charges were brought against Hillary Clinton by prosecutors based on evidence from Anthony Weiner's laptop. The article details how the FBI reviewed hundreds of thousands of emails found on the laptop but ultimately concluded that its investigation did not alter its previous decision not to recommend charges against Clinton, as stated in FBI Director Comey's November 6, 2016, letter to Congress.

Source URL: https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/hidden-legal-lessons-from-anthony-weiners-laptop-computer-stark

### 6. During the examination of Anthony Weiner's laptop, did authorities discover files or communications that they determined constituted evidence of Hillary Clinton's associates' involvement in child trafficking or pedophilia?
Answer: No, the FBI's examination of Anthony Weiner's laptop did not result in any official determination or charges that files or communications constituted evidence of Hillary Clinton or her associates' involvement in child trafficking or pedophilia. While allegations and claims from unnamed sources circulated in certain media and forums during the 2016 election, no authoritative law enforcement body has confirmed these claims.

Source URL: https://www.nairaland.com/3448812/nypd-source-weiner-laptop-enough

### 7. Have any associates of Hillary Clinton been investigated, charged, or convicted of child trafficking or pedophilia based on evidence sourced from Anthony Weiner's laptop?
Answer: Based on the provided search result from True Pundit, there is no evidence presented that any specific associates of Hillary Clinton were investigated, charged, or convicted of child trafficking or pedophilia *as a direct result of evidence sourced from Anthony Weiner's laptop*. # Claim 80 — “Red-tagging” is a term invented by the Communist Party of the Philippines, the New People's Army, and the National Democratic Front (CPP-NPA-NDF).

## Initial Q&A

### 1. What is the established definition of the political term "Red-tagging"?
Answer: According to Philippine jurisprudence, "red-tagging" is defined as "the act of labelling, branding, naming and accusing individuals and/or organizations of being left-leaning, subversives, communists or terrorists (used as) a strategy… by state agents, particularly law enforcement agencies and the military, against those perceived to be ‘threats’ or ‘enemies’ of the state."

Source URL: https://www.philstar.com/headlines/2019/10/07/1958232/how-activists-respond-being-tagged-rebels

### 2. In what year or time period did the term "Red-tagging" first appear in documented public or political discourse?
Answer: Based on the provided source, the term "Red-tagging" first appeared in documented public or political discourse in 2011, as it was defined in a journal that year and subsequently cited in a 2015 Supreme Court dissenting opinion.

Source URL: https://www.wikiwand.com/en/Red-tagging_in_the_Philippines

### 3. What is the earliest known published source (e.g., document, newspaper, interview) that uses the exact term "Red-tagging"?
Answer: Based on the provided search result, the earliest known published source using the exact term "Red-tagging" referenced within the article is the **House Bill No. 9199 (the proposed Human Rights Defender Protection Act), approved on June 3, 2019**, which included a government obligation to refrain from "red-tagging." However, the article does not provide a direct quote from the bill's text, so the exact initial publication date or earlier sources are not specified here.

Source URL: https://opinion.inquirer.net/127085/red-tagging-in-up-diliman

### 4. Does the earliest known source for the term "Red-tagging" originate from a document or statement issued by the Communist Party of the Philippines, the New People's Army, or the National Democratic Front?
Answer: The search result indicates that "red-tagging" began in the Philippines in the mid-1950s and is historically associated with McCarthyist strategies used to label individuals as communists, but it does not attribute the term's origin to the Communist Party of the Philippines, the New People's Army, or the National Democratic Front. Instead, the article describes red-tagging as a tactic employed by governments and security forces against activists.

Source URL: https://www.bbc.com/news/world-asia-54144623

### 5. Are there historical records showing the term "Red-tagging" being used by political groups, academics, or government agencies in the Philippines prior to any known usage by the CPP-NPA-NDF?
Answer: Yes, historical records show the term and practice of "red-tagging" being used by political groups and government agencies in the Philippines prior to any known usage by the CPP-NPA-NDF, with the Wikipedia article noting it is "a relic of the Cold War" and has a long history in the Philippines, exemplified by the 1950s targeting of labor organizer Johnny Tan and others by the Committee on Un-Filipino Activities (CUFA).

Source URL: https://www.wikiwand.com/en/Red-tagging_in_the_Philippines

### 6. Have Filipino human rights organizations or civil society groups independently claimed to have coined or popularized the term "Red-tagging"?
Answer: The request was rejected because it was considered high risk

Source URL: http://www.indigenouspeoples-sdg.org/index.php/english/ttt/1369-chr-denounces-red-tagging-of-groups-individuals-amid-covid-19-crisis

### 9. Has the meaning or application of the term "Red-tagging" changed significantly since its first known use?
Answer: Yes, while the core meaning of red-tagging—publicly labeling individuals or groups as communist sympathizers to suppress dissent—has remained consistent, its application has significantly expanded over time. The practice is described as "a relic of the Cold War" with historical roots dating back to the 1950s, when figures like labor organizer Johnny Tan and nationalist politician Claro M. Recto were targeted by bodies such as the Committee on Un-Filipino Activities. However, the scope of red-tagging has broadened dramatically: it now targets an extensive range of groups including labor unions, religious institutions, journalists, lawyers, judges, artists, humanitarian organizations like Oxfam, community pantries set up during COVID-19, and even government agencies like the Commission on Human Rights and COMELEC. Additionally, its consequences have intensified, as the Anti-Terrorism Act of 2020 introduced severe legal penalties and expanded state powers of surveillance and detention, making red-tagged individuals more vulnerable to arrest, asset seizure, and violence—effects that Amnesty International and Karapatan have linked to extrajudicial killings.

Source URL: https://www.wikiwand.com/en/Red-tagging_in_the_Philippines

### 10. Do historians of the Philippine left movement confirm or refute the attribution of the term's invention to the CPP-NPA-NDF?
Answer: Historians refute the attribution, as historian Joseph Scalice states that red-tagging "began in the Philippines in the mid-1950s" as a state strategy linked to McCarthyism, predating the CPP-NPA-NDF.

Source URL: https://www.bbc.com/news/world-asia-54144623

# Claim 89 — Most deaths in the 1918 influenza pandemic originated from bacterial pneumonia caused by face masks and that Dr Anthony Fauci, the US government’s top expert in the fight against Covid-19, knew about it.

## Initial Q&A

### What was the primary cause of death during the 1918 influenza pandemic according to modern epidemiological and historical studies?
Answer: According to modern epidemiological and historical studies, the primary cause of death during the 1918 influenza pandemic was secondary bacterial pneumonia. As summarized in a key study, "The majority of deaths in the 1918–1919 influenza pandemic likely resulted directly from secondary bacterial pneumonia caused by common upper respiratory-tract bacteria."

Source URL: https://academic.oup.com/jid/article/198/7/962/2192118

### Is there any scientific or historical evidence from studies of the 1918 pandemic that directly links the use of face masks to increased rates of bacterial pneumonia?
Answer: Based on the provided source, there is no evidence linking the use of face masks to increased rates of bacterial pneumonia during the 1918 pandemic. The article explains that bacterial pneumonia was the primary cause of death, but it occurred as a secondary infection following the influenza virus, which damaged the respiratory tract and facilitated bacterial invasion. The source does not mention face masks as a contributing factor.

Source URL: https://www.newscientist.com/article/dn14458-bacteria-were-the-real-killers-in-1918-flu-pandemic/

### During the 1918 influenza pandemic, was the widespread adoption of face masks empirically shown to cause secondary bacterial infections or pneumonia?
Answer: The search result provides extensive evidence that secondary bacterial pneumonia was the primary cause of death during the 1918 influenza pandemic but contains no information linking the use of face masks to these infections. The study discusses the biological mechanism where the influenza virus damaged the respiratory tract, allowing common bacteria to invade, but it does not mention face masks as a contributing factor.

Source URL: https://www.cidrap.umn.edu/influenza-vaccines/study-bacterial-pneumonia-was-main-killer-1918-flu-pandemic

### Have any reputable medical or historical sources, such as autopsy studies or epidemiological analyses, concluded that bacterial pneumonia was the main cause of death in the 1918 pandemic?
Answer: Yes, reputable medical sources have concluded that bacterial pneumonia was the main cause of death in the 1918 pandemic. A key study published in *The Journal of Infectious Diseases* by Morens, Taubenberger, and Fauci (2008) examined autopsy data and historical records, concluding that "the majority of deaths in the 1918–1919 influenza pandemic likely resulted directly from secondary bacterial pneumonia caused by common upper respiratory-tract bacteria."

Source URL: https://academic.oup.com/jid/article/198/7/962/2192118

### Is the theory that face masks caused bacterial pneumonia deaths in 1918 a widely accepted conclusion in the medical or historical community, or is it a fringe or contested view?
Answer: The search result does not support the claim that face masks caused bacterial pneumonia deaths in 1918. It confirms that bacterial pneumonia following influenza infection was the leading cause of death, but it attributes this to the virus destroying cells in the respiratory tract, allowing bacteria from the nose and throat to invade the lungs. The accepted medical and historical consensus, as reflected in this NIH-supported research, centers on the biological sequence of viral damage followed by secondary bacterial infection—not on mask use as a cause. The theory presented in the claim is not indicated as part of mainstream scientific or historical understanding.

Source URL: https://www.nih.gov/news-events/news-releases/bacterial-pneumonia-caused-most-deaths-1918-influenza-pandemic

### Are there any published peer-reviewed articles or official reports from the 1918 era or from modern research that document face masks as a primary vector for the pathogens causing fatal bacterial pneumonia?
Answer: The search result does not provide direct evidence from peer-reviewed studies or official reports documenting face masks as a primary vector for the pathogens causing fatal bacterial pneumonia during the 1918 pandemic. While the paper references historical data indicating that most 1918 deaths were due to secondary bacterial pneumonia and speculates that masking may have increased fatality, it does not cite any specific studies from 1918 or modern research that empirically establish masks as the primary transmission route for those pathogens. Instead, the paper argues for potential risks of microbial contamination from masks based on modern experiments and general historical anecdotes, without directly linking masks to the 1918 bacterial pneumonia deaths as a documented cause.

Source URL: https://pdmj.org/masks2/Mask_Risks_Part2.pdf

### Has the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) or other public health agencies ever stated that face masks used during the 1918 pandemic led to bacterial pneumonia and increased mortality?

# Claim 99 — New Zealand's Abortion Legislation Act (2020) does not make it a legal requirement that babies born alive after an abortion are given medical support.

## Initial Q&A

### 1. What are the exact provisions of New Zealand's Abortion Legislation Act 2020 regarding the standard of care and legal obligations for a live birth that occurs as a result of a failed abortion procedure?
Answer: The Abortion Legislation Act 2020 does not contain specific provisions regarding medical support for babies born alive after a failed abortion. During the bill's passage, an amendment by National MP Simon O'Connor that would have required "medical intervention of unintended live births after attempted termination" was considered but rejected by Parliament with a vote of 37–80. The Act itself primarily focuses on decriminalizing abortion and regulating access, rather than establishing protocols for live births resulting from the procedure.

Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_Legislation_Act_2020

### 2. How does the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 define or classify the status of a fetus or infant born alive following a termination of pregnancy?
Answer: The provided search result does not contain information on how the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 defines or classifies the status of a fetus or infant born alive following a termination.

Source URL: https://simple.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_Legislation_Act_2020

### 3. Does the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 explicitly create, modify, or repeal any legal duty for healthcare practitioners to provide medical treatment to a live-born infant in any context, including after a failed abortion?
Answer: Based on the provided source, the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 does not explicitly create, modify, or repeal any legal duty for healthcare practitioners to provide medical treatment to a live-born infant. This is evidenced by the fact that Parliament voted down the "Care of child born after abortion" amendment, which "would have required... a duty to provide the child with appropriate medical care and treatment," indicating the final Act contains no such new statutory duty.

Source URL: https://righttolife.org.uk/news/nz-pm-jacinda-ardern-votes-against-born-alive-amendment

### 7. Has there been any documented case in New Zealand since 2020 where a healthcare provider was investigated, charged, or disciplined for their actions or inaction regarding the care of an infant born alive after an abortion?
Answer: The search result does not contain any information about documented cases, investigations, charges, or disciplinary actions against healthcare providers in New Zealand regarding the care of infants born alive after an abortion since 2020.

Source URL: https://fli.org.nz/life-faith-family-resources/abortion/nz-abortion-law/

### 8. What were the stated policy intentions and legislative debates surrounding the relevant clauses in the Abortion Legislation Act 2020, particularly regarding the care for a live birth following a termination?
Answer: The stated policy intention behind the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 was to decriminalize abortion and regulate it as a health matter, but legislative debates explicitly addressed the care for a live birth following a termination. A key amendment proposed by MP Simon O'Connor, which would have required health professionals to provide medical care to a baby born alive after a failed abortion "no different than the duty to provide medical care and treatment to a child born other than as a result of an abortion," was debated and rejected by Parliament, with a vote of 80 against to 37 in favor.

Source URL: https://bothlivesmatter.org.nz/the-law-in-detail/

### 9. How does the legal framework established by the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 compare to the legal obligations that existed under the previous legislation (the Contraception, Sterilisation, and Abortion Act 1977) regarding this specific scenario?
Answer: The search result indicates that under the previous New Zealand law (the Contraception, Sterilisation, and Abortion Act 1977), late-term abortions post-20 weeks were permitted only on very strict grounds—"necessary to save the life of the woman or girl or to prevent serious permanent injury to her physical or mental health"—which meant such abortions were rare (e.g., only 72 in 2017). By contrast, when the Abortion Legislation Bill was being debated in 2020, an amendment proposed by MP Simon O'Connor that would have explicitly required health professionals to provide medical care and treatment to babies born alive after failed abortions—"no different than the duty to provide medical care and treatment to a child born other than as a result of an abortion"—was voted down by 81 MPs, including Prime Minister Jacinda Ardern. While the search result does not explicitly detail whether the 1977 Act had a specific born-alive provision, it strongly implies that the previous restrictive framework around late-term abortions made the scenario far less likely, whereas the 2020 Act's expansion of access to later-term abortions (allowing abortion on broader grounds between 20 weeks and birth) made it more likely—yet Parliament declined to impose any legal obligation to provide medical support to any resulting live births.

Source URL: https://righttolife.org.uk/news/nz-pm-jacinda-ardern-votes-against-born-alive-amendment

### 10. Do official interpretations from the New Zealand government or judiciary clarify that the absence of a specific mandate in the Abortion Legislation Act 2020 means there is no legal requirement, or that the requirement is sourced elsewhere in law?
Answer: The search result contains a direct official interpretation through the legislative process: during the Bill's Committee stage, Parliament explicitly rejected an amendment by National MP Simon O'Connor "requiring medical intervention of unintended live births after attempted termination," voting it down by 37–80. This rejection by Parliament constitutes an official interpretation that the Act's silence is intentional, confirming there is no legal requirement sourced within the Act itself.

Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_Legislation_Act_2020

# Claim 105 — President Trump said Anthony Fauci said, “This is not a threat. This is not a problem. Don’t worry about it.” when talking about the pandemic

## Initial Q&A

### Did Dr. Anthony Fauci ever make a public or recorded statement containing the exact words "This is not a threat. This is not a problem. Don't worry about it." in reference to the COVID-19 pandemic?
Answer: No, Dr. Anthony Fauci did not make such a statement with those exact words. According to FactCheck.org, in a Feb. 29 interview, Fauci said that "right now at this moment" the risk was "low" and there was "no need" for people "to change anything that you're doing on a day-by-day basis," but he also warned that "this could change," that people needed to be wary of "community spread," and that it could develop into a "major outbreak." Trump's characterization that Fauci said "This is no problem. This is going to blow over" was a misquotation, and the exact phrasing "This is not a threat. This is not a problem. Don't worry about it." does not appear in any recorded Fauci statement found in this source.

Source URL: https://www.factcheck.org/2020/04/trump-misquotes-fauci-on-coronavirus-threat/

# Claim 110 — Fact check: Harris' statement on Lincoln's Supreme Court nomination misses key details

## Initial Q&A

### What is the exact and complete statement made by Kamala Harris regarding a Supreme Court nomination by Abraham Lincoln?
Answer: During the vice presidential debate, Kamala Harris stated: "In 1864... Abraham Lincoln was up for reelection. And it was 27 days before the election. And a seat became open on the United States Supreme Court. Abraham Lincoln's party was in charge not only of the White House but the Senate. But Honest Abe said, 'It's not the right thing to do. The American people deserve to make the decision about who will be the next president of the United States, and then that person will be able to select who will serve on the highest court of the land.'"

Source URL: https://www.foxnews.com/media/washington-post-fact-checks-harris-history-lincoln

### On what date and in what forum (e.g., speech, debate, tweet) did Kamala Harris make the statement about Lincoln's Supreme Court nomination?
Answer: Kamala Harris made the statement about Lincoln's Supreme Court nomination during the **first vice presidential debate on October 7, 2020**, in response to a question from Vice President Mike Pence about whether she and Joe Biden would increase the number of justices on the Supreme Court.

Source URL: https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/factcheck/2020/10/13/fact-check-harris-lesson-lincoln-supreme-court-nom-lacks-detail/5943183002/

### Which specific Supreme Court justice was Abraham Lincoln nominating at the time of the event referenced in Harris's statement?
Answer: According to the source, when Chief Justice Roger Taney died in 1864, "Lincoln appointed his former Treasury Secretary to succeed him." This refers to **Salmon P. Chase**, Lincoln's former Treasury Secretary, whom Lincoln nominated to replace Taney as Chief Justice in 1864—an election year—which is the event typically referenced in Harris's statement about Lincoln's Supreme Court nomination timing.

Source URL: https://www.abrahamlincolnonline.org/lincoln/education/supreme.htm

### What were the complete circumstances and political context surrounding Lincoln's Supreme Court nomination in question?
Answer: Lincoln's 1864 Supreme Court nomination of Salmon P. Chase occurred amidst the Civil War, with the seat opening just weeks before Lincoln's reelection bid. The political context was complex: Lincoln led a fragile coalition including War Democrats and faced a general election challenge from George B. McClellan. The search result clarifies that while Lincoln delayed the nomination for political reasons—specifically to use the vacancy as leverage to secure the campaigning support of Chase and his Radical Republican allies—the Senate's recess from July 4 to December 5, 1864, also made a pre-election confirmation physically impossible.

Source URL: https://www.snopes.com/fact-check/lincoln-1864-supreme-court/

### What specific historical facts or details did the fact-check identify as being "missed" or omitted from Kamala Harris's statement?
Answer: The fact-check identified that Kamala Harris omitted two key details: first, that the Senate was in recess and not in session at the time of the vacancy, making a nomination impossible until it reconvened; and second, that Lincoln's delay was primarily due to political strategy—not a principled decision to defer to voters. As reported, Lincoln "did so not to defer to voters — as Harris stated — but because the Senate was not in session. He submitted a nomination a day after Congress reconvened."

Source URL: https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/factcheck/2020/10/13/fact-check-harris-lesson-lincoln-supreme-court-nom-lacks-detail/5943183002/

### Which organization, publication, or individual authored the fact-check that claims Harris's statement misses key details?
Answer: The fact-check was authored by **The Washington Post**. As cited in the search result: "according to a report from The Washington Post on Thursday, Harris did not accurately describe what took place under Lincoln..."

Source URL: https://www.foxnews.com/media/washington-post-fact-checks-harris-history-lincoln

### What is the stated methodology or standard of evidence used by the fact-checker in their analysis of Harris's statement?
Answer: The fact-checker's stated methodology relies on historical research and expert analysis, including academic works, historian accounts, and official records, to verify the claim against documented events; they concluded by stating, "We rate this claim TRUE, based on our research," after evaluating evidence about Lincoln's actions and motivations.

Source URL: https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/factcheck/2020/10/13/fact-check-harris-lesson-lincoln-supreme-court-nom-lacks-detail/5943183002/

### Does Harris's statement, in its full context, make a direct factual claim that can be clearly proven true or false, or is it more of a rhetorical or interpretive comparison?
Answer: Harris's statement contains elements of both direct factual claims and interpretive/rhetorical framing. On the factual side, she made verifiable assertions such as the vacancy arising 27 days before the election and Lincoln's party controlling both the White House and the Senate, which are clearly provable true or false and were largely confirmed. However, her core narrative — that Lincoln deliberately delayed the nomination out of principle because "the American people deserve to make the decision" — was an interpretive or rhetorical characterization of his motivations, not a straightforward factual claim. Historians told fact-checkers that Lincoln's actual reasons were strategic and practical (the Senate was not in session, and he didn't want to alienate supporters or jeopardize his reelection), with one historian stating, "I've never seen anything like that" in reference to Harris's framing of Lincoln's motives. So while the factual scaffolding of her statement was mostly accurate, the central argument she built on it was more of an interpretive comparison that distorted the historical record by omitting key details.

Source URL: https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/factcheck/2020/10/13/fact-check-harris-lesson-lincoln-supreme-court-nom-lacks-detail/5943183002/

### How does the interpretation of Lincoln's nomination by professional historians compare to both Harris's statement and the fact-check's critique?
Answer: The search result does not directly cite professional historians, but its critique aligns with the likely historical consensus: while Harris's timeline is correct, her portrayal of Lincoln as deferring to voters' choice is unsupported, as he had other political motives for the delay.

Source URL: https://www.lincolncottage.org/the-washington-post-kamala-harriss-little-history-lesson-about-lincolns-supreme-court-vacancy-wasnt-exactly-true/

### Is there a pattern or precedent of this type of incomplete reference to Lincoln's nomination in political discourse, and does the fact-checker note this?
Answer: The fact-checker explicitly notes a pattern of incomplete reference to Lincoln's nomination in political discourse by pointing out that Kamala Harris's portrayal omits key details, such as Lincoln's political motivations and the procedural constraints of the time, stating that "Lincoln's having either expressed or acted upon this sentiment" is unsupported and that his delay was "politically expedient" rather than a principled stance.

Source URL: https://www.snopes.com/fact-check/lincoln-1864-supreme-court/

# Claim 112 — The Median White Household Wealth In The US Is 81 Times that of The Black Median Household Wealth

## Initial Q&A

### What is the most recent and authoritative source for data on median household wealth by race in the United States, such as the U.S. Federal Reserve's Survey of Consumer Finances?
Answer: The most recent and authoritative source is the U.S. Federal Reserve's 2019 Survey of Consumer Finances, with data analyzed in their 2020 report which states that "White families have more wealth than Black, Hispanic, and other or multiple race families," displaying "median (top panel) and mean (bottom panel) wealth by race and ethnicity."

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/notes/feds-notes/disparities-in-wealth-by-race-and-ethnicity-in-the-2019-survey-of-consumer-finances-accessible-20200928.htm

### According to the latest available data from the chosen authoritative source, what is the reported ratio of median white household wealth to median Black household wealth in the United States?
Answer: The search result does not provide the specific median wealth figures for white and Black households needed to calculate the ratio, but it states that in 2016, "the median or typical Black household reported about $43,000 (or 56 percent) less wealth than the median white household reported," using data from the Federal Reserve’s Survey of Consumer Finances. To determine the exact ratio, the underlying median values would be required.

Source URL: https://www.brookings.edu/articles/how-to-build-black-wealth/

### How does the authoritative source define "household wealth" or "net worth" for the purpose of calculating this ratio (e.g., assets minus debts, including home equity)?
Answer: According to the authoritative Federal Reserve analysis of the 2019 Survey of Consumer Finances, "Wealth is defined as the difference between families' gross assets and their liabilities," which is equivalent to net worth; however, the data shows the typical White family has **eight times** the wealth of the typical Black family, not 81 times, indicating the claim is inaccurate.

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/notes/feds-notes/disparities-in-wealth-by-race-and-ethnicity-in-the-2019-survey-of-consumer-finances-20200928.html

### Does the reported median wealth figure for each racial group represent the median for all households of that race, or does it apply to a subset (e.g., only households with positive net worth)?
Answer: The reported median wealth figures represent the median for all households of each racial group, including those with negative net worth, as the source defines wealth as "the difference between families' gross assets and their liabilities" without excluding households with negative net worth.

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/notes/feds-notes/disparities-in-wealth-by-race-and-ethnicity-in-the-2019-survey-of-consumer-finances-20200928.html

### Has the 81:1 ratio been directly and explicitly stated by the authoritative source (e.g., the Federal Reserve), or is it a calculation derived from reported median wealth figures?
Answer: The search result does not explicitly state the 81:1 ratio. Instead, it reports absolute differences in median wealth from the Federal Reserve's Survey of Consumer Finances, noting that in 2016, "the median or typical Black household reported about $43,000 (or 56 percent) less wealth than the median white household." This suggests the 81:1 ratio is a calculation derived from such reported median figures, not a direct quote from the source.

Source URL: https://www.brookings.edu/articles/how-to-build-black-wealth/

### What was the median white household wealth and the median Black household wealth in the most recent survey to calculate the precise ratio?
Answer: According to the 2019 Survey of Consumer Finances, the median White household wealth was **$188,200**, while the median Black household wealth was **$24,100**, resulting in a ratio of approximately **7.8 to 1** (or about eight times).

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/notes/feds-notes/disparities-in-wealth-by-race-and-ethnicity-in-the-2019-survey-of-consumer-finances-20200928.html

### How does the claimed 81:1 ratio compare to the ratios reported in previous years by the same source, indicating any recent trend (increasing, stable, or decreasing disparity)?
Answer: Based on the search result, the claimed 81:1 ratio is part of a trend of increasing disparity. The report states that between 1983 and 2013, median Black household wealth fell to $1,700 while median White wealth rose to $116,800, a ratio of about 68.7:1 in 2013. It then projects that by 2020, "White households are projected to own 85 times more wealth than Black households." Therefore, compared to the 2013 ratio of approximately 68.7:1, both the claimed 81:1 ratio and the projected 85:1 ratio indicate a recent and accelerating trend of increasing racial wealth disparity.

Source URL: https://prosperitynow.org/blog/black-and-latino-households-are-short-road-zero-wealth-hollowing-out-americas-historic-middle

### Are there other major, credible surveys or reports (e.g., from the U.S. Census Bureau or Pew Research Center) that produce a significantly different ratio for the same period?
Answer: Yes, according to the Federal Reserve's 2019 Survey of Consumer Finances, the typical White family had eight times the wealth of the typical Black family, a ratio significantly different from the claimed 81:1.

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/notes/feds-notes/disparities-in-wealth-by-race-and-ethnicity-in-the-2019-survey-of-consumer-finances-20200928.html

### What methodological notes or caveats does the authoritative source provide about interpreting the median wealth figures and the resulting racial wealth gap?
Answer: The authoritative source, a Federal Reserve working paper analyzing the Survey of Consumer Finances, provides several key methodological caveats. It notes that the racial wealth gap is "particularly large" at the median compared to the mean due to the high concentration of wealth at the top. Crucially, the authors caution that they do not "interpret the race coefficients in our OLS regressions as causal" and state that the "unexplained portion of the racial wage gap identified in the decomposition analysis" should not be considered "a proxy for current or past racial discrimination," as it includes all unobserved factors. They further emphasize that even the *explained* portion of the gap, driven by factors like income and homeownership, "could reflect themselves the effects of racial biases as well." The analysis also finds that the unexplained gap is not uniform, being "significantly higher at the top of the wealth distribution," while at the bottom, observable factors can completely account for the gaps.

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econresdata/feds/2015/files/2015076pap.pdf

# Claim 117 — NASA always receives blessings from the Pope and that God’s permission must be sought before a space mission.

## Initial Q&A

### 1. Has NASA ever issued an official policy, guideline, or regulation stating that a blessing from the Pope or permission from God is required before launching a space mission?
Answer: No, NASA has never issued an official policy, guideline, or regulation stating that a blessing from the Pope or permission from God is required before launching a space mission. The search result describes a Russian Orthodox blessing tradition that occurs at the Baikonur Cosmodrome, noting that American astronauts participate primarily because NASA relies on Russian launch facilities. As the source explains, "Ever since the Space Shuttle was retired in 2011, NASA has relied upon Russia's Soyuz rockets and its Baikonur Cosmodrome to transport men and women up to the International Space Station," indicating the practice is situational rather than a formal NASA requirement.

Source URL: https://www.theverge.com/2015/4/1/8324707/astronauts-blessing-nasa-space-baikonur

### 2. Does any U.S. federal law or regulation mandate that NASA seek religious approval, from the Vatican or any other entity, before conducting space operations?
Answer: No, the search result does not indicate that any U.S. federal law or regulation mandates NASA seek religious approval from the Vatican or any other entity before space operations. Instead, it highlights NASA's obligation to balance employees' religious freedoms with the Establishment Clause, ensuring no government endorsement of religion.

Source URL: https://www.catholic.org/news/hf/faith/story.php?id=67227

### 3. Can historical records, official mission logs, or public communications from NASA confirm that the agency requested and received a specific blessing from the Pope for each of its major missions (e.g., Apollo, Space Shuttle, Mars rovers, James Webb Space Telescope)?
Answer: No, historical records and official communications from NASA do not confirm that the agency systematically requested and received a specific blessing from the Pope for each major mission. The search result describes a unique 2011 event where Pope Benedict XVI spoke with astronauts aboard the Space Shuttle Endeavour and the International Space Station, noting it was "the first ever papal call to space." This instance was arranged by the European and Italian space agencies, with NASA providing technical support, and it was not presented as a standard pre-launch ritual. While Pope Paul VI sent a pre-recorded greeting on a disk left on the Moon by Apollo 11 in 1969, this was a goodwill message among many from world leaders, not a blessing sought or required by NASA for the mission.

Source URL: https://www.denverpost.com/2011/05/21/pope-blesses-astronauts-in-1st-papal-call-to-space-2/

### 4. Has the Vatican or the Pope ever publicly stated that it grants official permission to NASA for its space missions?
Answer: No, the search result does not indicate that the Vatican or the Pope has ever publicly stated that it grants official permission to NASA for its space missions. The article describes the Vatican's interest in ethical space exploration and its diplomatic role in United Nations space committees, but it frames the relationship as one of spiritual blessings and advocacy, not administrative approval.

Source URL: https://www.ncronline.org/earthbeat/out-world-vaticans-care-creation-includes-final-frontier

### 5. Are there documented instances where NASA delayed or cancelled a mission due to not receiving a blessing from the Pope or divine permission?
Answer: The search result describes a 2011 event where Pope Benedict XVI blessed astronauts during a space mission, but it was a communication event during an ongoing mission, not a prerequisite for launch. There is no mention in the source of any mission being delayed, cancelled, or operationally impacted by a papal blessing or lack thereof.

Source URL: https://www.nbcnews.com/id/wbna43120039

### 7. What are the established, secular protocols and approval processes (e.g., Presidential Directive, Federal Aviation Administration launch license) that NASA must legally follow before a mission, and do any of them include a religious component?
Answer: NASA's established approval processes are entirely secular and legally mandated, involving presidential directives, congressional oversight, and regulatory bodies such as the Federal Aviation Administration for launch licensing. The search result details advisory structures like the NASA Advisory Council, which provides "consensus advice" under the Federal Advisory Committee Act, with members "chosen by NASA" and "serve at the pleasure of the NASA Administrator." These processes involve no religious components, as they are grounded in U.S. government and safety protocols.

Source URL: https://www.nasa.gov/nac/

### 8. Have other major space agencies (e.g., ESA, Roscosmos, JAXA) ever publicly referenced a similar requirement to seek blessings from the Pope or divine permission for their missions?
Answer: No, the search result describes a one-time interfaith conversation and blessing exchange between Pope Francis and the Expedition 53 crew (which included astronauts from ESA and Roscosmos), but it does not indicate that any space agency has a public requirement or official protocol to seek such blessings for missions.

Source URL: https://blogs.nasa.gov/spacestation/2017/10/26/pope-francis-and-expedition-53-crew-exchange-thoughts-about-humanitys-deepest-and-oldest-questions/

### 10. Has the claim been previously investigated and reported on by reputable, secular news organizations or fact-checking agencies, and what were their conclusions?

The article also notes that the call "was arranged by the European Space Agency and the Italian Space Agency," with NASA merely providing "technical support," and makes no mention of any requirement to seek "God's permission" before a space mission. This evidence suggests the claim is false: the Pope does not "always" bless NASA missions, and there is no protocol of seeking divine permission for space missions.

# Claim 119 — The PHE has began to combine Covid-19 and influenza statistics to make the numbers look worse.

## Initial Q&A

### 1. Did Public Health England (PHE) officially announce or implement a policy of combining COVID-19 and influenza statistics into a single reporting category?
Answer: No, the search result does not show that Public Health England (PHE) implemented a policy of combining COVID-19 and influenza statistics into a single reporting category. The cited document is a "Weekly National Influenza & COVID-19 Report" which monitors and presents surveillance data for both viruses **separately**, such as distinct case counts, positivity rates, and hospital admission rates, rather than merging them into one statistic.

Source URL: https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/government/uploads/system/uploads/attachment_data/file/925324/Weekly_Flu_and_COVID-19_report_W41_FINAL.pdf

### 3. What was the stated reason or public health justification provided by PHE for any decision to combine COVID-19 and influenza statistics?
Answer: Based on the provided document, Public Health England's stated reason for adapting and combining COVID-19 and influenza surveillance systems was to effectively monitor the emerging pandemic and understand its impact on public health. The report explains that PHE "created new surveillance systems and adapted existing influenza surveillance systems" to track SARS-CoV-2, which was necessary because "COVID-19 has impacted on various influenza indicators presented in this report." The document presents this adaptation as a practical, methodological response to a novel health threat, not as an effort to manipulate data.

Source URL: http://allcatsrgrey.org.uk/wp/download/public_health/epidemiology/Surveillance_Influenza_and_other_respiratory_viruses_in_the_UK_2019_to_2020_FINAL.pdf

### 4. Is there any official documentation, press release, or published methodology from PHE confirming that COVID-19 and influenza statistics were combined?
Answer: No, the search result—the comprehensive PHE surveillance report for Winter 2019 to 2020—does not contain any official documentation, press release, or published methodology confirming that COVID-19 and influenza statistics were combined. Instead, the report consistently distinguishes between the two diseases and notes that PHE "created new surveillance systems and adapted existing influenza surveillance systems" specifically for COVID-19, publishing separate weekly reports. It does warn that some influenza indicators were impacted by the COVID-19 pandemic, but this is presented as a methodological caution, not as a confirmation of a deliberate combination of statistics.

Source URL: http://allcatsrgrey.org.uk/wp/download/public_health/epidemiology/Surveillance_Influenza_and_other_respiratory_viruses_in_the_UK_2019_to_2020_FINAL.pdf

### 5. Did PHE ever explicitly state or imply that the purpose of combining COVID-19 and influenza data was to make COVID-19 appear more severe or dangerous?
Answer: No, in the provided search result, Public Health England (PHE) did not state or imply that combining COVID-19 and influenza data was intended to make the virus appear more severe. Instead, PHE’s warnings focused on the genuine, research-based risk of higher mortality from co-infection with both viruses to justify expanding the flu vaccination program as a public health measure.

Source URL: https://www.independent.co.uk/news/uk/politics/coronavirus-flu-death-risk-public-health-england-vaccine-jonathan-vantam-b517342.html

### 6. Do other national or international public health agencies (such as the U.S. CDC, WHO, or European CDC) also track or report combined respiratory illness data, and if so, for what stated reasons?
Answer: Yes, other major international public health agencies, such as the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and the WHO Regional Office for Europe, do track and report combined respiratory illness data. They have jointly launched the European Respiratory Virus Surveillance Summary (ERVISS) for influenza, RSV, and SARS-CoV-2, with the stated primary aim "to serve as a tool for the early detection and communication of signals of respiratory virus circulation."

Source URL: https://flunewseurope.org/

### 7. Is there a legitimate epidemiological or public health reason for monitoring combined respiratory illness statistics (e.g., tracking overall respiratory disease burden)?
Answer: Yes, there is a legitimate public health reason. Monitoring combined respiratory illness statistics, such as COVID-19 and influenza, allows health authorities to track the overall burden of respiratory diseases on the healthcare system and inform public health responses. For example, Manitoba's public health unit explains they produce a "combined respiratory virus surveillance report" that includes "surveillance indicators and intelligence around COVID-19, influenza and other respiratory pathogens," indicating this integrated approach is a standard practice for comprehensive disease monitoring.

Source URL: https://www.gov.mb.ca/health/publichealth/surveillance/covid-19/index.html

### 8. When, according to the claim, did PHE begin combining these statistics, and does this timeline align with PHE's actual reporting practices?
Answer: The claim does not specify when PHE began combining the statistics, but the search result explains that from 29 April 2020, PHE provided new data for DHSC that combined deaths from COVID-19 across three sources (hospitals, outbreak management, and laboratory-confirmed tests) to include all confirmed COVID-19 deaths regardless of setting. This change aimed to improve transparency and completeness, not to combine COVID-19 with influenza data, which is not mentioned in the source.

Source URL: https://www.ons.gov.uk/news/statementsandletters/thedifferentusesoffiguresondeathsfromcovid19publishedbydhscandtheons

### 10. Does the original source of this claim provide verifiable evidence (such as screenshots of data, links to PHE reports, or testimony from PHE officials) to support the assertion?
Answer: The search result does not address whether the original source of the claim provided verifiable evidence such as screenshots of data, links to PHE reports, or testimony from PHE officials to support the specific assertion that PHE combined Covid-19 and flu statistics to inflate numbers. The article discusses PHE's warnings about the dangers of co-infection and the expansion of the flu vaccination programme, but it does not provide any information about the sourcing or evidence behind the claim in question.

Source URL: https://www.independent.co.uk/news/uk/politics/coronavirus-flu-death-risk-public-health-england-vaccine-jonathan-vantam-b517342.html

# Claim 120 — A law called The Flora and Fauna Act classified aboriginal people as animals until Australian voters overturned it in the 1960s.

## Initial Q&A

### Was there ever a single piece of Australian federal or state legislation officially titled "The Flora and Fauna Act"?
Answer: No. According to extensive research documented in the source, there was never any Australian federal or state legislation officially titled "The Flora and Fauna Act." The article's author states: "More than a year after posting, there is no evidence that there ever was a Flora and Fauna Act anywhere in Australia. There was certainly no Commonwealth act." Commenters also confirmed they could not find such an Act in any jurisdiction. Prominent Indigenous scholar Marcia Langton further clarified that the claim "is a myth that has circulated in the Aboriginal movement since the 1970s. It started as a joke and I think it was Lester or Jerry Bostock who first described our status with this phrase at a FCAATSI conference in Canberra in 1977. It was an attempt to say that at that time we were regarded as less than animals." While real and deeply harmful discriminatory legislation existed—such as various state "protection" acts, the exclusion of Aboriginal people from the census (Section 127 of the Constitution), and the amalgamation of Aboriginal affairs with departments like "Aborigines and Fisheries" in Western Australia—no Act by the specific name "The Flora and Fauna Act" ever existed.

Source URL: https://clubtroppo.com.au/2013/06/03/were-indigenous-people-regulated-by-the-flora-fauna-act/

### Did any Australian law explicitly define Aboriginal Australians as "flora" or "fauna" within its legal text?
Answer: No Australian law ever explicitly defined Aboriginal Australians as "flora" or "fauna." As researcher Samuel Byrnand concluded after extensive investigation: "The Flora and Fauna Act does not exist now, nor has it ever existed. It is a very powerful and successful example of the urban myth." The claim is a persistent and widely believed piece of misinformation that has gained traction due to high-profile repetition and the plausibility given Australia's well-documented history of racist legislation and mistreatment of Aboriginal people, but no such legal text has ever been found.

Source URL: https://www.sbs.com.au/nitv/article/shareena-clanton-is-like-many-of-us-a-victim-of-the-flora-and-fauna-act-myth/m40dgtgah

### What was the legal classification of Aboriginal Australians under Australian federal and state laws prior to 1967?
Answer: Prior to the 1967 referendum, Aboriginal Australians were **not** classified as flora or fauna under any federal or state law. The claim that a "Flora and Fauna Act" existed is a persistent myth. As the ABC Fact Check investigation confirmed, "Aboriginal people in Australia have never been covered by a flora and fauna act, either under federal or state law." Before 1967, Aboriginal people were governed primarily by state laws rather than Commonwealth laws, and Section 127 of the Australian Constitution excluded them from being counted in the national population for constitutional purposes. While they were not formally classified as animals, experts acknowledged that discriminatory treatment was pervasive; as Professor Marcia Langton noted, "We were not classified under the 'flora and fauna act' but we were treated as animals." The myth likely arose from factors such as government department names (e.g., Western Australia's "Department of Aborigines and Fisheries"), a 1929 royal commission chapter heading grouping "Aborigines, Fauna and Flora," and the symbolic narrative built around the 1967 referendum by yes campaigners who framed it as granting equal rights.

Source URL: https://www.abc.net.au/news/2018-03-20/fact-check-flora-and-fauna-1967-referendum/9550650

### In what year did Australian citizens vote in a national referendum that pertained to the rights or classification of Aboriginal Australians?
Answer: Australian citizens voted in the national referendum pertaining to Aboriginal rights and constitutional recognition in **1967**.

Source URL: https://web.library.uq.edu.au/blog/2017/05/1967-referendum

### What was the primary question or constitutional change put to voters in the 1967 Australian referendum?
Answer: The primary question put to voters in the 1967 Australian referendum was whether to amend the Constitution to (1) remove the words "other than the aboriginal race in any State" from Section 51, clause 26, thereby allowing the Commonwealth government to make laws for Aboriginal people, and (2) repeal Section 127, which excluded Aboriginal people from being counted in the national population. The actual referendum question read: "Do you approve the proposed law for the alteration of the Constitution entitled 'An Act to alter the Constitution' so as to omit certain words relating to the people of the Aboriginal race in any state so that Aboriginals are to be counted in reckoning the population?" Voters approved the change overwhelmingly, making it the largest "yes" vote in Australian referendum history.

Source URL: https://www.creativespirits.info/aboriginalculture/history/australian-1967-referendum

### Did the 1967 Australian referendum directly repeal or modify any existing act named "The Flora and Fauna Act"?
Answer: No. The 1967 referendum amended the Australian Constitution by passing the Constitution Alteration (Aboriginals) 1967, which removed discriminatory clauses in sections 51(xxvi) and 127. It did not directly repeal or modify any act named "The Flora and Fauna Act"; indeed, the source makes no mention of any such act at all. The referendum's effect was to "allow Indigenous people to be included in the census" and give "federal Parliament the power to make laws in relation to Indigenous people."

Source URL: https://web.library.uq.edu.au/blog/2017/05/1967-referendum

### What specific legal changes regarding Aboriginal Australians were achieved through the 1967 referendum or other legislative acts in the 1960s?
Answer: The 1967 referendum achieved two specific constitutional changes: first, it removed the words "…other than the aboriginal people in any State…" from Section 51(xxvi), thereby giving the federal Parliament the power to make laws in relation to Indigenous people; second, it removed Section 127 entirely, which had stated that "aboriginal natives should not be counted" in reckoning population numbers, thus allowing Indigenous Australians to be included in the census. The constitutional amendment was enacted through the *Constitution Alteration (Aboriginals) 1967*, and an Office of Aboriginal Affairs was established as a result. However, as the source notes, the referendum "did not give Aboriginal people the right to vote" (which had existed since 1962) and "did not grant them citizenship," as most discriminatory federal and state laws had already been repealed by that time. Despite the overwhelming success of the vote (90% in favor), the Holt Government announced it had no intention of introducing further measures to assist Aboriginal people, and substantive improvements did not come until the Whitlam Government was elected in 1972.

Source URL: https://web.library.uq.edu.au/blog/2017/05/1967-referendum

### Who or which organization is the common source for the claim about a "Flora and Fauna Act" classifying Aboriginal people as animals?
Answer: The common source for the claim about a "Flora and Fauna Act" is the Aboriginal movement, where it originated as a rhetorical statement or joke in the 1970s. As Aboriginal activist and scholar Marcia Langton stated, "The story … is a myth that has circulated in the Aboriginal movement since the 1970s. It started as a joke and I think it was Lester or Jerry Bostock who first described our status with this phrase at a FCAATSI conference in Canberra in 1977."

Source URL: https://clubtroppo.com.au/2013/06/03/were-indigenous-people-regulated-by-the-flora-fauna-act/

### What are the primary historical records or legal documents that would prove or disprove the existence of such an act with that specific name and clause?
Answer: According to research conducted by Samuel Byrnand, a political science lecturer at the University of Canberra specializing in Australian political and legislative history, extensive investigation into the historical record yielded "mostly questions and dead ends in lieu of hard corroborating evidence." As Byrnand states, "The Flora and Fauna Act does not exist now, nor has it ever existed. It is a very powerful and successful example of the urban myth." No such act appears in any legislative record, statute book, or archival document. The primary documents that *do* exist in Australian legislative history—such as records of the Aboriginal Protection Board, various state Aboriginal welfare/protection acts, and the 1967 Referendum materials—contain no reference to Aboriginal people being classified as flora and fauna. The myth appears to have gained traction through conflations with the 1967 Referendum (which actually amended sections 51 and 127 of the Australian Constitution regarding the Commonwealth's power to make laws for Aboriginal people and their inclusion in population counts) and the broader, well-documented history of legislated racism, such as the White Australia Policy and various Aboriginal protection statutes. The absence of any such act in the official compendium of Commonwealth or state legislation—easily verifiable through the Federal Register of Legislation or state parliamentary archives—constitutes definitive disproof of its existence.

Source URL: https://www.sbs.com.au/nitv/article/shareena-clanton-is-like-many-of-us-a-victim-of-the-flora-and-fauna-act-myth/m40dgtgah

### What were the main laws or constitutional provisions that defined the legal status of Aboriginal Australians before the 1960s, and how were they amended or repealed?
Answer: Before the 1960s, the legal status of Aboriginal Australians was defined primarily by two provisions in the Australian Constitution: **Section 51(xxvi)**, which excluded "the aboriginal people in any State" from the Commonwealth's power to make special laws for any race, effectively preventing the Commonwealth from legislating on behalf of Aboriginal people; and **Section 127**, which stated that "aboriginal natives should not be counted" in reckoning the numbers of the people of the Commonwealth, thereby excluding Aboriginal people from the census. Additionally, the **Commonwealth Franchise Act 1902** specifically excluded Indigenous people from voting, and Indigenous Australians were denied social security benefits throughout the first half of the 20th century. These provisions were addressed through the **1967 referendum**, held on 27 May 1967, in which nearly 91 per cent of voters approved constitutional amendments that removed the exclusionary language in both sections—allowing Aboriginal people to be counted in the census and giving the Commonwealth the power to make laws with respect to Aboriginal people. The constitutional changes were formally enacted on 10 August 1967, and were followed by the passage of the **States Grants (Aboriginal Advancement) Act 1968**, which provided federal funds to support Indigenous communities.

Source URL: https://www.nma.gov.au/defining-moments/resources/indigenous-referendum
